  ✔ row-level security: every messaging table is forced, the worker reads only its scope, no role can delete a thread, task or message (61.211526ms)
✔ messaging (BE-19) on PostgreSQL 17 (4353.93439ms)

▶ staff alerts and the alert stream on PostgreSQL 17 (BE-07)
  ▶ migration rules (22.13, ADR-022)
    ✔ T-STAFF fails closed until BE-08: a patient-bound alert is invisible to staff, an alert without a patient is not (28.821197ms)
    ✔ install the T-STAFF stand-in (patient.staff_scope arrives with BE-08): the test replaces ONE function (1.100737ms)
    ✔ M-5 patient guard: an alert for an unregistered patient is still refused (PM008) and leaves no row (3.281492ms)
    ✔ ADR-022: admin-realm alert types never carry a patient ID, in the service and in the table (11.948355ms)
    ✔ state rules: closed is final, status moves forward only, targets are only added, one event of each kind (13.976212ms)
    ✔ every new table has FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY and no policy matches anonymous or public (4.025419ms)
  ✔ migration rules (22.13, ADR-022) (64.220586ms)
  ▶ escalatesAt and escalationLevel (TC-PC-16 steps 1-2, AL-02)
    ✔ null rule: no configured time -> null in list, open-ids, stream and no job; configured -> equal in all three places (106.268637ms)
    ✔ the clinical setting red_flag_escalation_minutes is the fallback for red_flag; the alert-setting row wins; other types have none (14.117347ms)
    ✔ settings are validated and admin-realm only (6.512518ms)
  ✔ escalatesAt and escalationLevel (TC-PC-16 steps 1-2, AL-02) (127.510303ms)
  ▶ acknowledge (TC-PC-16 steps 3-4)
    ✔ two simultaneous acknowledgements both get 200 with exactly one acknowledged event; escalatesAt null after (66.176658ms)
    ✔ the same Idempotency-Key replays the first answer; a reused key for another alert conflicts; the key is required (58.033107ms)
    ✔ a principal who is not a target gets 404 kernel.not_found, with no side effect (25.904139ms)
    ✔ HEAAA-286 (draft 8.3): a red flag targeted at a principal holding the seeded doctor role is listed, streamed and acknowledged; a doctor who is not targeted gets 404 and sees nothing (38.2 item 22) (86.120986ms)
    ✔ an admin-realm principal (compliance) reads and acknowledges an alert of a compliance type in its own scope; no patient anywhere (12.480215ms)
  ✔ acknowledge (TC-PC-16 steps 3-4) (249.223489ms)
  ▶ escalation job (32.4, TC-PC-16 step 5)
    ✔ the job does nothing after an acknowledge (no escalated event, no status change, nothing published) (24.964325ms)
    ✔ an unacknowledged alert escalates once: level 1, status escalated, escalate_at cleared, one event, event published; a repeat does nothing (97.361502ms)
    ✔ acknowledge racing the escalation job: one consistent outcome, one event of each kind (117.064929ms)
    ✔ an alert without a patient escalates in an org scope; the job refuses a staff scope and a bad level (22.538731ms)
  ✔ escalation job (32.4, TC-PC-16 step 5) (262.418825ms)
  ▶ targets (ADR-022) and row-level security
    ✔ role and permission targets are resolved to principals at creation, de-duplicated; unresolvable targets are refused (13.113853ms)
    ✔ a rolled-back alert leaves no row, no event, no job and sends no notification (307.431574ms)
    ✔ the NOTIFY payload is IDs only, below 8,000 bytes, and broadcast beyond 100 targets (62.601143ms)
    ✔ RLS: the creator of a red flag reads it back in her own transaction but never in a tray; a non-target reads nothing; staff cannot forge other types (29.571044ms)
    ✔ the anonymous and patient audiences read and write nothing (5.276267ms)
  ✔ targets (ADR-022) and row-level security (418.607034ms)
  ▶ the alert stream (38.2 item 6, ARCHITECTURE 32)
    ✔ an outdated, malformed or missing clientRelease is refused with a plain 400 before any event; a current one connects (15.302483ms)
    ✔ replay on reconnect in seq order after Last-Event-ID; live alerts through the real LISTEN/NOTIFY; alert_closed to the other target (767.525926ms)
    ✔ event data is the closed field list: no PHI, no free text (SEC-01 ST-2); the patient ID never appears (43.917983ms)
    ✔ resync instead of a replay when more than 200 alerts were missed (597.118407ms)
    ✔ more than 3 streams per session: the oldest is closed with event: closed; the 10 per principal limit holds (95.598702ms)
    ✔ kill switch: session_revoked closes the principal's streams at once with a final event: closed (15.309175ms)
    ✔ ST-3: with the LISTEN connection dropped (fault injection) a revoked session's stream still closes within the bound; the stream then catches up (590.462218ms)
    ✔ an alert raised while the LISTEN connection is down reaches an open stream after the hub reconnects (a lost NOTIFY delays an alert, never loses it) (523.051504ms)
    ✔ an idle-expired session's stream closes at the next session check (123.289505ms)
    ✔ a draining instance closes every stream with event: closed (10.121625ms)
  ✔ the alert stream (38.2 item 6, ARCHITECTURE 32) (2782.560279ms)
  ▶ merge and export contribution (22.16)
    ✔ R states re-point, closed is kept, counts add up, events are kept; only a patients-scope job naming the IDs may run it (25.596481ms)
  ✔ merge and export contribution (22.16) (25.779655ms)

▶ notifications on PostgreSQL 17 (BE-06, API side)
  ▶ templates (22.13, RA-R1-05, BD-10)
    ✔ a template binding a service, diagnosis, medicine or clinician name cannot be saved - service layer and database (22.373089ms)
    ✔ render check then publish: needs the check, needs every configured language, publishes the whole version, retires the old one (112.513633ms)
    ✔ only the admin realm writes templates; staff and patients read them (T-CONF); anonymous reads none (12.745482ms)
  ✔ templates (22.13, RA-R1-05, BD-10) (148.519911ms)
  ▶ send, dedupe, cancel (22.13, 24, BR-08)
    ✔ send binds allowlisted values only: one queued row per channel, a job per row in the same transaction, no address, no text (26.435086ms)
    ✔ free text, unknown names, a service or clinician field, bad links and condition-revealing location names never reach a notification (TC-NT-06) (14.061544ms)
    ✔ dedupe keys prevent double sends: a repeat creates nothing and queues nothing, also under concurrency (40.202456ms)
    ✔ a rolled-back business transaction leaves no notification and no job; a delayed send waits for send_after (21.997893ms)
    ✔ cancel by dedupe-key prefix cancels queued rows of that resource only; sent rows are final (43.30409ms)
    ✔ in-app notices: a patient lists only their own sent notices and marks them read once (17.446398ms)
  ✔ send, dedupe, cancel (22.13, 24, BR-08) (506.961565ms)
  ▶ row-level security and grants
    ✔ app_api: patients see only their own in-app rows; staff (google) and admin see no patient notification; anonymous sees nothing (9.263615ms)
    ✔ inserts: a patient only for themselves, queued only; admin realm only to principals; a worker needs its patients scope (14.97119ms)
    ✔ the application role cannot change content, delete, or touch receipts; receipts and reads are append-only (10.378618ms)
    ✔ table rules: dedupe key without the recipient, recipient kind consistent, values on the allowlist, forward-only status (9.396604ms)
  ✔ row-level security and grants (44.348872ms)
  ▶ merge and export participants (22.16, 24, SBR-16)
    ✔ repointPatient: R rows move to the survivor, K rows stay, counts add up for notifications, receipts and reads; scope is enforced (25.915701ms)
    ✔ exportPart returns the patient's notifications, receipts and reads - no address and no message text exist to leak (4.44686ms)
    ✔ the participants register with the kernel registries once (0.606198ms)
  ✔ merge and export participants (22.16, 24, SBR-16) (31.216715ms)
✔ notifications on PostgreSQL 17 (BE-06, API side) (2455.562229ms)
